<ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:OrganizationConsolidationAndPresentationOfFinancialStatementsDisclosureTextBlock" id="f-345" continuedAt="f-345-1" escape="true">Organization and Basis of Presentation</ix:nonNumeric></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:24.75pt"><span><br/></span></div><ix:continuation id="f-345-1"><ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:BasisOfAccountingPolicyPolicyTextBlock" id="f-346" escape="true"><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Organization and Nature of Operations</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Magnolia Oil &amp; Gas Corporation (either individually or together with its consolidated subsidiaries, as the context requires, the &#8220;Company&#8221; or &#8220;Magnolia&#8221;) is an independent oil and natural gas company engaged in the acquisition, development, exploration, and production of oil, natural gas, and natural gas liquid (&#8220;NGL&#8221;) reserves. The Company&#8217;s oil and natural gas properties are located primarily in the Karnes and Giddings areas in South Texas where the Company primarily targets the Eagle Ford Shale and Austin Chalk formations. Magnolia&#8217;s objective is to generate stock market value over the long term through steady organic production growth, high full cycle operating margins, an efficient capital program with short economic paybacks, significant free cash flow after capital expenditures, and effective reinvestment of free cash flow.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Basis of Presentation</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The accompanying unaudited consolidated financial statements have been prepared in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles in the United States of America (&#8220;GAAP&#8221;) and the rules and regulations of the Securities and Exchange Commission (&#8220;SEC&#8221;) for interim financial reporting. Accordingly, certain disclosures normally included in an Annual Report on Form 10-K have been omitted. The consolidated financial statements and related notes included in this Quarterly Report should be read in conjunction with the Company&#8217;s consolidated financial statements and related notes included in the Company&#8217;s Annual Report on Form 10-K for the period ended&#160;December&#160;31, 2025 (the &#8220;2025 Form 10-K&#8221;). Except as disclosed herein, there have been no material changes to the information disclosed in the notes to the consolidated financial statements included in the Company&#8217;s 2025 Form 10-K.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">In the opinion of management, all normal, recurring adjustments and accruals considered necessary to present fairly, in all material respects, the Company&#8217;s interim financial results have been included. Operating results for the periods presented are not necessarily indicative of expected results for the full year.</span></div></ix:nonNumeric><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%"><ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:PriorPeriodReclassificationAdjustmentDescription" id="f-347" escape="true">Certain reclassifications of prior period financial statements have been made to conform to current reporting practices.</ix:nonNumeric> <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:ConsolidationPolicyTextBlock" id="f-348" escape="true">The consolidated financial statements include the accounts of the Company and its subsidiaries after elimination of intercompany transactions and balances. The Company&#8217;s interests in oil and natural gas exploration and production ventures and partnerships are proportionately consolidated. The Company reflects a noncontrolling interest representing the interest owned by the Magnolia LLC Unit Holders through their ownership of Magnolia LLC Units in the consolidated financial statements. The noncontrolling interest is presented as a component of equity.</ix:nonNumeric> See&#160;</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%"><a style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%;text-decoration:none" href="#i658097c8de274e8d8a7ff749ae5c7cb8_64">Note </a>10&#8212;Stockholders&#8217; Equity</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">&#160;for further discussion of the noncontrolling interest.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:SegmentReportingPolicyPolicyTextBlock" id="f-349" escape="true"><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Segment Information</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company operates in <ix:nonFraction unitRef="segment" contextRef="c-1" decimals="INF" name="us-gaap:NumberOfReportableSegments" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-350">one</ix:nonFraction> reportable segment engaged in the acquisition, development, exploration, and production of oil and natural gas properties (&#8220;Operating segment&#8221;). Magnolia&#8217;s operations are conducted predominantly in <ix:nonFraction unitRef="area" contextRef="c-1" decimals="INF" name="mgy:NumberOfGeographicalAreasOperationsAreConducted" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-351">one</ix:nonFraction> geographic area of the United States. The Operating segment sells oil, natural gas, and NGLs which are disaggregated on the Company&#8217;s consolidated statements of operations. The profit or loss metric used to evaluate segment performance is net income reported on the Company&#8217;s consolidated statements of operations. The measure of segment assets is reported on the Company&#8217;s consolidated balance sheets as Total Assets. Significant segment expenses are the same as those in the consolidated statements of operations.</span></div></ix:nonNumeric></ix:continuation><div The Company has concluded that disaggregating revenue by product type appropriately depicts how the nature, amount, timing, and uncertainty of revenue and cash flows are affected by economic factors and has reflected this disaggregation of revenue on the Company&#8217;s consolidated statements of operations for all periods presented. The Company&#8217;s receivables consist mainly of trade receivables from commodity sales and joint interest billings due from owners on properties the Company operates. Receivables from contracts with customers totaled $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-3" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:ContractWithCustomerAssetNetCurrent" scale="6" id="f-355">160.8</ix:nonFraction> million as of March&#160;31, 2026 and $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-4" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:ContractWithCustomerAssetNetCurrent" scale="6" id="f-356">116.5</ix:nonFraction> million as of December&#160;31, 2025. style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">5. <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:FairValueDisclosuresTextBlock" id="f-360" continuedAt="f-360-1" escape="true">Fair Value Measurements</ix:nonNumeric></span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><ix:continuation id="f-360-1" continuedAt="f-360-2"><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Certain of the Company&#8217;s assets and liabilities are carried at fair value and measured either on a recurring or nonrecurring basis. The Company&#8217;s fair value measurements are based either on actual market data or assumptions that other market participants would use in pricing an asset or liability in an orderly transaction, using the valuation hierarchy prescribed by GAAP under Accounting Standards Codification (&#8220;ASC&#8221;) 820.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The three levels of the fair value hierarchy under ASC 820 are as follows: </span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Level 1 - Quoted prices (unadjusted) in active markets for identical investments at the measurement date are used.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Level 2 - Pricing inputs are other than quoted prices included within Level 1 that are observable for the investment, either directly or indirectly. Level 2 pricing inputs include quoted prices for similar investments in active markets, quoted prices for identical or similar investments in markets that are not active, inputs other than quoted prices that are observable for the investment, and inputs that are derived principally from or corroborated by observable market data by correlation or other means.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Level 3 - Pricing inputs are unobservable and include situations where there is little, if any, market activity for the investment. The inputs used in determination of fair value require significant judgment and estimation.</span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company has other financial instruments consisting primarily of receivables, payables, and other current assets and liabilities that approximate fair value due to the nature of the instruments and their relatively short maturities. Non-financial assets and liabilities initially measured at fair value include assets acquired and liabilities assumed in business combinations and asset retirement obligations.</span></div></ix:continuation><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">6</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><ix:continuation id="f-360-2"><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Recurring Fair Value Measurements</span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Long-Term Debt</span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The fair value of the Senior Notes at March&#160;31, 2026 and December&#160;31, 2025 was $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-74" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:LongTermDebtFairValue" scale="6" id="f-361">413.1</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million and $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-75" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:LongTermDebtFairValue" scale="6" id="f-362">412.4</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million, respectively. The fair value is based on unadjusted quoted prices in an active market, which is considered a Level 1 input in the fair value hierarchy. The carrying value of the Senior Notes, net of unamortized deferred financing costs, was $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-76" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:LongTermDebt" scale="6" id="f-363">393.4</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million and $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-77" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:LongTermDebt" scale="6" id="f-364">393.3</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of March&#160;31, 2026 and December&#160;31, 2025, respectively, and is included in &#8220;Long-term debt, net&#8221; on the Company&#8217;s consolidated balance sheets.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Liability-Classified Stock Based Compensation</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">The fair value of the liability for future cash-settled stock based compensation was $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-78" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:DeferredCompensationSharebasedArrangementsLiabilityCurrentAndNoncurrent" scale="6" id="f-365">7.4</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million and $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-79" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:DeferredCompensationSharebasedArrangementsLiabilityCurrentAndNoncurrent" scale="6" id="f-366">5.6</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of March&#160;31, 2026 and December&#160;31, 2025, respectively, and is included in &#8220;Other current liabilities&#8221; and &#8220;Other long-term liabilities&#8221; on the Company&#8217;s consolidated balance sheets. The fair value of the liability for future cash-settled stock based compensation is estimated using observable market data (the total shareholder return (&#8220;TSR&#8221;) of the Class A Common Stock relative to the TSR achieved by a specific industry peer group) and Monte Carlo simulation models, which is considered a Level 2 input in the fair value hierarchy. </span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Nonrecurring Fair Value Measurements</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Certain of the Company&#8217;s assets and liabilities are measured at fair value on a nonrecurring basis. Specifically, equity-classified stock based compensation is not measured at fair value on an ongoing basis but is subject to fair value calculations in certain circumstances. For further detail, see </span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Note 11&#8212;Stock Based Compensation</span><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">. There were no other material nonrecurring fair value measurements as of March&#160;31, 2026 or December&#160;31, 2025.</span></div></ix:continuation><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div id="i658097c8de274e8d8a7ff749ae5c7cb8_52"></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Additionally, Magnolia Operating is required to pay a commitment fee quarterly in arrears in respect of unused commitments under the RBL Facility. The applicable margin and the commitment fee rate are calculated based upon the utilization levels of the RBL Facility as a percentage of unused lender commitments then in effect. The RBL Facility contains certain affirmative and negative covenants customary for financings of this type, including compliance with a leverage ratio of less than <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-82" decimals="INF" name="mgy:DebtInstrumentCovenantLeverageRatio" scale="0" id="f-393">3.50</ix:nonFraction> to 1.00 and a current ratio of greater than <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-82" decimals="INF" name="mgy:DebtInstrumentCovenantCurrentRatio" scale="0" id="f-394">1.00</ix:nonFraction> to 1.00. The unamortized portion of the deferred financing costs is included in &#8220;Other long-term assets&#8221; on the Company&#8217;s consolidated balance sheets as of March&#160;31, 2026 and December&#160;31, 2025. </span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span In some instances, the Company may be directed to suspend or cease operations in an affected area. The Company estimates its annual effective tax rate in recording its quarterly provision for income taxes in the various jurisdictions in which it operates. The Company&#8217;s effective tax rates for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 and 2025 were <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-1" decimals="3" name="us-gaap:EffectiveIncomeTaxRateContinuingOperations" scale="-2" id="f-426">17.2</ix:nonFraction>% and <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-15" decimals="3" name="us-gaap:EffectiveIncomeTaxRateContinuingOperations" scale="-2" id="f-427">19.1</ix:nonFraction>%, respectively. The primary differences between the annual effective tax rates and the statutory rate of 21.0% are state taxes, tax credits, and income attributable to noncontrolling interest.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">On July 4, 2025, the U.S. enacted legislation referred to as the One Big Beautiful Bill Act, which contains certain significant changes to U.S. corporate income tax laws and is generally effective for tax years beginning after December 31, 2024. These changes include, among others, the immediate deduction of domestic research and development (&#8220;R&amp;D&#8221;) expenses, the option to retroactively deduct previously capitalized R&amp;D expenses, and 100% bonus depreciation for property acquired after January 19, 2025. The impacts are reflected in the Company&#8217;s income tax provision for the quarter ended March 31, 2026, which resulted in a decrease in current tax expense offset by an increase in deferred tax expense.</span></div></ix:continuation><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div id="i658097c8de274e8d8a7ff749ae5c7cb8_64"></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">10. <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-1" name="us-gaap:StockholdersEquityNoteDisclosureTextBlock" id="f-428" continuedAt="f-428-1" escape="true">Stockholders&#8217; Equity </ix:nonNumeric></span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><ix:continuation id="f-428-1" continuedAt="f-428-2"><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Class A Common Stock </span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:24.75pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">At March&#160;31, 2026, there were <ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-6" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:CommonStockSharesIssued" scale="6" id="f-429">234.3</ix:nonFraction> million shares of Class A Common Stock issued and <ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-6" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:CommonStockSharesOutstanding" scale="6" id="f-430">185.4</ix:nonFraction> million shares of Class A Common Stock outstanding. The holders of Class A Common Stock vote together as a single class&#160;on all matters and are entitled <ix:nonFraction unitRef="vote" contextRef="c-6" decimals="INF" name="mgy:CommonStockVotingRightsNumberOfVotesPerShare" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-431"><ix:nonFraction unitRef="vote" contextRef="c-7" decimals="INF" name="mgy:CommonStockVotingRightsNumberOfVotesPerShare" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-432">one</ix:nonFraction></ix:nonFraction> </span></div></ix:continuation><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">9</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><ix:continuation id="f-428-2"><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">vote for each share held. There is no cumulative voting with respect to the election of directors, which results in the holders of more than <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-3" decimals="2" name="mgy:ThresholdForHoldersOfCompanyCommonStockToBeAbleToElectAllDirectors" scale="-2" id="f-433">50</ix:nonFraction>% of the Company&#8217;s outstanding common shares being able to elect all of the directors. In the event of a liquidation, dissolution, or winding up of the Company, the holders of the Class A Common Stock are entitled to share ratably in all assets remaining available for distribution to them after payment of liabilities and after provision is made for each class of stock, if any, having preference over the common stock. The holders of the Class A Common Stock have no preemptive or other subscription rights, and there are no sinking fund provisions applicable to such shares.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Class B Common Stock and Noncontrolling Interest</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:24.75pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">In February 2026, the Magnolia LLC Unit Holders redeemed <ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-93" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:StockRedeemedOrCalledDuringPeriodShares" scale="6" id="f-434"><ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-94" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:StockRedeemedOrCalledDuringPeriodShares" scale="6" id="f-435">4.8</ix:nonFraction></ix:nonFraction> million Magnolia LLC Units (and a corresponding number of shares of Class B Common Stock) for an equivalent number of shares of Class A Common Stock and such shares of Class A Common Stock were subsequently sold by the Magnolia LLC Unit Holders to the public. As of March&#160;31, 2026, the aforementioned transactions eliminated the Company&#8217;s noncontrolling interest and Magnolia owned <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-97" decimals="3" name="us-gaap:VariableInterestEntityOwnershipPercentage" scale="-2" id="f-440">100.0</ix:nonFraction>% of the interest in Magnolia LLC. </span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="padding-left:36pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Share Repurchase Program</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, the Company&#8217;s board of directors had authorized a share repurchase program of up to <ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-98" decimals="INF" name="srt:StockRepurchaseProgramNumberOfSharesAuthorizedToBeRepurchased" scale="6" id="f-441">60.0</ix:nonFraction> million shares of Class A Common Stock. amended, the &#8220;Plan&#8221;), effective as of July 17, 2018. A total of <ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-6" decimals="INF" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardNumberOfSharesAuthorized" scale="6" id="f-473">16.8</ix:nonFraction> million shares of Class A Common Stock have been authorized for issuance </span></div></ix:continuation><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">10</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><ix:continuation id="f-472-2" continuedAt="f-472-3"><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">under the Plan as of March&#160;31, 2026. The Company grants stock based compensation awards in the form of restricted stock units (&#8220;RSU&#8221;), performance restricted stock units (&#8220;PRSU&#8221;), and performance share units (&#8220;PSU&#8221;) to eligible employees and directors to enhance the Company&#8217;s ability to attract, retain, and motivate persons who make important contributions to the Company by providing these individuals with equity ownership opportunities. Shares issued as a result of awards granted under the Plan are generally new shares of Class A Common Stock. The Company&#8217;s awards provide for accelerated vesting upon retirement under specific conditions.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Stock based compensation expense is recognized net of forfeitures within &#8220;General and administrative expenses&#8221; and &#8220;Lease operating expenses&#8221; on the consolidated statements of operations and was $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-1" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:AllocatedShareBasedCompensationExpense" scale="6" id="f-474">12.2</ix:nonFraction> million and $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-15" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:AllocatedShareBasedCompensationExpense" scale="6" id="f-475">6.5</ix:nonFraction> million for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 and 2025, respectively. The Company has elected to account for forfeitures of awards granted under the Plan as they occur in determining compensation expense. The total income tax benefit recognized for stock that vested during the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 and 2025 was $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-1" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:EmployeeServiceShareBasedCompensationTaxBenefitFromCompensationExpense" scale="6" id="f-476">5.7</ix:nonFraction> million and $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-15" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:EmployeeServiceShareBasedCompensationTaxBenefitFromCompensationExpense" scale="6" id="f-477">3.4</ix:nonFraction> million, respectively.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">On February 12, 2025, certain PSUs were modified to be <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-106" decimals="2" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardPercentageOfEquityInstrumentsOtherThanOptionsModifiedSettledInCash" scale="-2" id="f-478">50</ix:nonFraction>% settled in cash. In accordance with ASC 718, the Company reclassified <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-106" decimals="2" name="mgy:PercentageOfImpactedAwardsReclassifiedFromEquityToLiability" scale="-2" id="f-479">50</ix:nonFraction>% of the impacted PSUs from equity-classified awards to liability-classified awards, resulting in a reclassification of $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-106" decimals="-5" name="mgy:AdjustmentsToAdditionalPaidInCapitalReclassificationOfAwardsFromEquityToLiability" scale="6" id="f-480">2.0</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million from equity to liability. The modification resulted in additional compensation expense of $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-106" decimals="-5" name="mgy:ShareBasedPaymentArrangementPlanModificationAdditionalCompensationExpense" scale="6" id="f-481">0.4</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million recognized within &#8220;General and administrative expenses&#8221; on the consolidated statements of operations. The modification affected <ix:nonFraction unitRef="grantee" contextRef="c-106" decimals="INF" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardPlanModificationNumberOfEmployeesAffected" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-482">three</ix:nonFraction> grantees.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Restricted Stock Units </span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company grants service-based RSU awards to employees, which generally vest and settle ratably over a <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-119" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingPeriod1" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-525">three-year</ix:nonNumeric> service period, and to non-employee directors, which vest in full after <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-120" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingPeriod1" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-526">one year</ix:nonNumeric>. Non-employee directors may elect to defer the RSU settlement date. RSUs represent the right to receive shares of Class A Common Stock at the end of the vesting period equal to the number of RSUs that vest. RSUs are subject to restrictions on transfer and are generally subject to a risk of forfeiture if the award recipient ceases to be an employee or director of the Company prior to vesting of the award. Compensation expense for the service-based RSU awards is based upon the grant date market value of the award and such costs are recorded on a straight-line basis over the requisite service period for each separately vesting portion of the award, as if the award was, in-substance, multiple awards. The aggregate fair values of RSUs that vested during the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 and 2025 were $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-110" decimals="-5" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardEquityInstrumentsOtherThanOptionsVestedInPeriodFairValue" scale="6" id="f-527">20.2</ix:nonFraction> million and $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-117" decimals="-5" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardEquityInstrumentsOtherThanOptionsVestedInPeriodFairValue" scale="6" id="f-528">10.9</ix:nonFraction> million, respectively. Unrecognized compensation expense related to unvested RSUs as of March&#160;31, 2026 was $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-113" decimals="-5" name="us-gaap:EmployeeServiceShareBasedCompensationNonvestedAwardsTotalCompensationCostNotYetRecognizedShareBasedAwardsOtherThanOptions" scale="6" id="f-529">32.8</ix:nonFraction> million, which the Company expects to recognize over a weighted average period of <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-110" name="us-gaap:EmployeeServiceShareBasedCompensationNonvestedAwardsTotalCompensationCostNotYetRecognizedPeriodForRecognition1" format="ixt-sec:duryear" id="f-530">2.2</ix:nonNumeric> years.</span></div></ix:continuation><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">11</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><ix:continuation id="f-472-3" continuedAt="f-472-4"><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Performance Restricted Stock Units and Performance Share Units</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company previously granted PRSUs to certain employees. Each PRSU represents the contingent right to receive <ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-121" decimals="INF" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardNumberOfSharesIssuableUponVestingOfEachAward" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-531">one</ix:nonFraction> share of Class A Common Stock once the PRSU is both vested and earned. PRSUs generally vest and settle either ratably over a <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-111" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingPeriod1" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-532">three-year</ix:nonNumeric> service period or at the end of a <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-111" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingPeriod1" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-533">three-year</ix:nonNumeric> service period, in each case, subject to the recipient&#8217;s continued employment or service through each applicable vesting date. Each PRSU is earned based on whether Magnolia&#8217;s stock price achieves a target average stock price for any <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-111" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardMilestoneNumberOfConsecutiveTradingDays" format="ixt-sec:durday" id="f-534">20</ix:nonNumeric> consecutive trading days during the <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-111" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardPerformancePeriod" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-535">five-year</ix:nonNumeric> performance period (&#8220;Performance Condition&#8221;). If PRSUs are not earned by the end of the <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-111" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardPerformancePeriod" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-536">five-year</ix:nonNumeric> performance period, the PRSUs will be forfeited and no shares of Class A Common Stock will be issued, even if the vesting conditions have been met. Compensation expense for the PRSU awards is based upon the grant date fair market value of the award, calculated using a Monte Carlo simulation, and such costs are recorded on a straight-line basis over the requisite service period for each separately vesting portion of the award, as if the award was, in-substance, multiple awards, as applicable. The aggregate fair value of PRSUs that vested during the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025 was $<ix:nonFraction unitRef="usd" contextRef="c-122" decimals="-5" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardEquityInstrumentsOtherThanOptionsVestedInPeriodFairValue" scale="6" id="f-537">5.4</ix:nonFraction> million. The aggregate fair value of PRSUs that vested during the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 and the unrecognized compensation expense related to unvested PRSUs as of March&#160;31, 2026 were insignificant.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company grants equity-classified PSUs to certain employees. Each equity-classified PSU, to the extent earned, represents the contingent right to receive <ix:nonFraction unitRef="shares" contextRef="c-123" decimals="INF" name="mgy:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardNumberOfSharesIssuableUponVestingOfEachAward" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" scale="0" id="f-538">one</ix:nonFraction> share of Class A Common Stock and the awardee may earn between <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-124" decimals="INF" name="us-gaap:SharebasedCompensationArrangementBySharebasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingRightsPercentage" format="ixt:fixed-zero" scale="0" id="f-539">zero</ix:nonFraction> and <ix:nonFraction unitRef="number" contextRef="c-125" decimals="INF" name="us-gaap:SharebasedCompensationArrangementBySharebasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingRightsPercentage" scale="-2" id="f-540">200</ix:nonFraction>% of the target number of the equity-classified PSUs granted based on the total shareholder return (&#8220;TSR&#8221;) of the Class A Common Stock relative to the TSR achieved by a specific industry peer group over a <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-126" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingPeriod1" format="ixt-sec:durwordsen" id="f-541">three-year</ix:nonNumeric> performance period. In addition to satisfaction of the TSR conditions, vesting of the equity-classified PSUs is subject to the awardee&#8217;s continued employment through the date of settlement of the equity-classified PSUs (unless an employee elects to retire under certain qualifying conditions), which will occur within <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="c-126" name="mgy:SharebasedCompensationArrangementbySharebasedPaymentAwardSettlementDatePeriodFollowingPerformancePeriod" format="ixt-sec:durday" id="f-542">60</ix:nonNumeric> days following the end of the performance period. Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company&#8217;s unvested share-based payment awards that contain nonforfeitable rights to dividends or dividend equivalents (whether paid or unpaid) are deemed participating securities, and therefore dividends and net income allocated to such awards have been deducted from earnings in computing basic and diluted net income per share under the two-class method. The Company&#8217;s oil and natural gas properties are located primarily in the Karnes and Giddings areas in South Texas, where the Company primarily targets the Eagle Ford Shale and the Austin Chalk formations. Magnolia&#8217;s objective is to generate stock market value over the long term through consistent organic production growth, high full cycle operating margins, an efficient capital program with short economic paybacks, significant free cash flow after capital expenditures, and effective reinvestment of free cash flow. The Company&#8217;s allocation of capital prioritizes reinvesting in its business to achieve moderate and predictable annual volume growth balanced with returning capital to its shareholders through dividends and share repurchases.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Magnolia&#8217;s business model prioritizes prudent and disciplined capital allocation, free cash flow, and financial stability. The Company&#8217;s ongoing plan is to spend within cash flow on drilling and completing wells while maintaining low financial leverage. The Company&#8217;s gradual and measured approach toward the development of the Giddings area has created operating efficiencies leading to higher production.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Market Conditions Update</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Commodity prices continue to experience volatility driven by geopolitical conflict, evolving global supply-demand dynamics, and macroeconomic uncertainty. Most notably, the escalation of military conflict involving Iran has materially disrupted global energy markets, including significantly constraining the movement of global crude oil and refined product exports through the Strait of Hormuz. These developments, together with the ongoing Russia-Ukraine conflict, OPEC and OPEC+ production decisions, and changes in sanctions and trade restrictions affecting major oil-producing countries such as Russia, Iran, and Venezuela, have increased the risk of supply interruptions and contributed to substantial price volatility and uncertainty in global energy markets.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:115%">The macroeconomic and geopolitical environment remains uncertain and continues to evolve. In combination with geopolitical risks &#8212; including sanctions regimes, trade restrictions, tariff policies that remain subject to legal, regulatory, and policy uncertainty, and the potential for prolonged or expanded disruptions to global energy supply chains &#8212; these conditions continue to increase uncertainty with respect to commodity prices, operating costs, and capital availability. The Company continues to closely monitor developments in geopolitical conditions, international trade relations, tariff policies, and energy market dynamics, any of which could adversely affect operating results, financial condition, and future cash flows.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">15</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Business Overview</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, Magnolia&#8217;s assets in South Texas included 60,187 gross (39,935 net) acres in the Karnes area, and 741,586 gross (561,950 net) acres in the Giddings area. As of March&#160;31, 2026, Magnolia held an interest in approximately 2,890 gross (1,960 net) wells, with total production of 102.6 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Magnolia recognized net income attributable to Class A Common Stock of $99.8&#160;million, or $0.54 per diluted common share, for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026. Magnolia recognized net income of $100.8&#160;million, which includes noncontrolling interest of $1.0&#160;million related to the Magnolia LLC Units (and corresponding shares of Class B Common Stock) held by certain affiliates of EnerVest, for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">During the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026, the Company declared and paid cash dividends and distributions totaling $31.4&#160;million.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, the Company&#8217;s board of directors had authorized a share repurchase program of up to 60.0 million shares of Class A Common Stock. The program does not require purchases to be made within a particular timeframe. The Company had repurchased 48.4 million shares under the program at a cost of $945.4 million and had 11.6 million shares of Class A Common Stock remaining under its share repurchase authorization as of March&#160;31, 2026.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span 0;text-align:right;vertical-align:bottom"/></tr></table></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Lease operating expenses are costs incurred in the operation of producing properties, including expenses for utilities, direct labor, water disposal, workover rigs, workover expenses, materials, and supplies. Lease operating expenses for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 were $0.7 million higher, and $0.25 per boe lower, than the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025. The increase was due to an increase in surface repair and maintenance and compression associated with higher well count. The decrease in lease operating expenses per boe was due to higher production.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:112%">Gathering, transportation and processing (&#8220;GTP&#8221;) costs are costs incurred to deliver oil, natural gas, and NGLs to the market. These expenses can vary based on the volume of oil, natural gas, and NGLs produced as well as the cost of commodity processing. The GTP costs for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 were $3.3 million, or $0.25 per boe, higher, than the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025 driven by higher production and changes to certain gathering and processing contracts, which resulted in a higher portion of Magnolia&#8217;s GTP costs being recognized as expense versus a reduction to Magnolia&#8217;s natural gas revenues.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Taxes other than income include production, ad valorem, and franchise taxes. These taxes are based on rates primarily established by state and local taxing authorities. Production taxes are based on the market value of production. Ad valorem taxes are based on the fair market value of the mineral interests or business assets. Taxes other than income for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 were $3.7 million, or $0.53 per boe, lower than the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025. The decrease in taxes other than income was primarily due to a decrease in ad valorem taxes as a result of lower market valuations and a decrease in production taxes as a result of severance tax refunds.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">18</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Depreciation, depletion and amortization (&#8220;DD&amp;A&#8221;) during the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 was $7.5 million, or $0.10 per boe, higher than the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025. The increase in DD&amp;A was primarily due to higher production. The slight increase in the DD&amp;A rate period over period was primarily due to acquisitions made during 2026.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">General and administrative expenses (&#8220;G&amp;A&#8221;) consists primarily of salaries and related benefits, stock based compensation, office rent, legal and consulting fees, system costs and other administrative costs incurred. the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025 driven by an $8.8 million decrease in current income tax expense and offset by a $4.6 million increase in deferred income tax expense. The Company&#8217;s primary uses of cash have been for development of the Company&#8217;s oil and natural gas properties, returning capital to shareholders, bolt-on acquisitions of oil and natural gas properties, and general working capital needs.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">The Company may also utilize borrowings under other various financing sources available to Magnolia, including the RBL Facility and the issuance of equity or debt securities through public offerings or private placements, to fund Magnolia&#8217;s acquisitions and long-term liquidity needs. Magnolia&#8217;s ability to complete future offerings of equity and debt securities and the timing of these offerings will depend upon various factors, including prevailing market conditions and the Company&#8217;s financial condition. The Company anticipates its current cash balance, cash flows from operations, and its available sources of liquidity to be sufficient to meet the Company&#8217;s cash requirements. </span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:13.5pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, the Company had $400.0 million of principal debt related to the Senior Notes outstanding and no outstanding borrowings related to the RBL Facility. As of March&#160;31, 2026, the Company had $574.4 million of liquidity comprised of the $450.0 million of borrowing capacity under the RBL Facility, and $124.4 million of cash and cash equivalents.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:13.5pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">19</span></div></div></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Cash and Cash Equivalents</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:13.5pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">At March&#160;31, 2026, Magnolia had $124.4 million of cash and cash equivalents.&#160;The Company&#8217;s cash and cash equivalents are maintained with various financial institutions in the United States. Deposits with these institutions may exceed the amount of insurance provided on such deposits. However, the Company regularly monitors the financial stability of its financial institutions and believes that the Company is not exposed to any significant default risk.</span></div><div style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">During the first quarter of 2026, Magnolia operated two rigs. The Company&#8217;s ongoing plan is to continue to spend within cash flow on drilling and completing wells while maintaining low financial leverage.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Capital Requirements</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:24.75pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">As of March&#160;31, 2026, the Company&#8217;s board of directors had authorized a share repurchase program of up to 60.0 million shares of Class A Common Stock. The program does not require purchases to be made within a particular time frame and whether the Company undertakes these additional repurchases is ultimately subject to numerous considerations, market conditions, and other factors. During each of the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 and 2025, the Company repurchased 1.2 million and 2.2 million shares for a total cost of approximately $32.1 million and $52.0 million, respectively. </span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">During the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025, Magnolia LLC repurchased and subsequently canceled 0.7 million Magnolia LLC Units with an equal number of shares of corresponding Class B Common Stock for $19.8 million of cash consideration. As of March&#160;31, 2026, Magnolia owned 100.0% of the interest in Magnolia LLC.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">During the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026, the Company declared and paid cash dividends to holders of its Class A Common Stock totaling $30.5&#160;million. Additionally, $0.9&#160;million was distributed to the Magnolia LLC Unit Holders. During the three months ended March&#160;31, 2025, the Company declared and paid cash dividends to holders of its Class A Common Stock totaling $28.9&#160;million. Additionally, $0.8&#160;million was distributed to the Magnolia LLC Unit Holders. The amount and frequency of future dividends is subject to the discretion of the Company&#8217;s board of directors and primarily depends on earnings, capital expenditures, debt covenants, and various other factors.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div id="i658097c8de274e8d8a7ff749ae5c7cb8_88"></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Item 3. Quantitative and Qualitative Disclosures About Market Risk</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Interest Rate Risk</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">For variable rate debt, interest rate changes generally do not affect the fair market value of such debt, but do impact future earnings and cash flows, assuming other factors are held constant. The Company is subject to market risk exposure related to changes in interest rates on borrowings under the RBL Facility. Interest on borrowings under the RBL Facility is based on the SOFR rate or alternative base rate plus an applicable margin. At March&#160;31, 2026, the Company had no borrowings outstanding under the RBL Facility. </span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Commodity Price Risk</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">Magnolia&#8217;s primary market risk exposure is to the prices it receives for its oil, natural gas, and NGL production. The prices the Company ultimately realizes for its oil, natural gas, and NGLs are based on a number of variables, including prevailing index prices attributable to the Company&#8217;s production and certain differentials to those index prices. Pricing for oil, natural gas, and NGLs has historically been volatile and unpredictable, and this volatility is expected to continue in the future. The prices the Company receives for production depend on factors outside of its control, including physical markets, supply and demand, financial markets, and national and international policies. A $1.00 per barrel increase (decrease) in the weighted average oil price for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 would have increased (decreased) the Company&#8217;s revenues by approximately $14.6 million on an annualized basis and a $0.10 per Mcf increase (decrease) in the weighted average natural gas price for the three months ended March&#160;31, 2026 would have increased (decreased) the Company&#8217;s revenues by approximately $7.0 million on an annualized basis.</span></div><div style="height:36pt;position:relative;width:100%"><div style="bottom:0;position:absolute;width:100%"><div style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">21</span></div></div></div><div id="i658097c8de274e8d8a7ff749ae5c7cb8_91"></div><hr style="page-break-after:always"/><div style="min-height:36pt;width:100%"><div><span><br/></span></div></div><div style="margin-top:18pt;text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Item 4. Controls and Procedures</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Evaluation of Disclosure Controls and Procedures</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">As required by Rule 13a-15(b) under the Exchange Act, Magnolia has evaluated, under the supervision and with the participation of the Company&#8217;s management, including Magnolia&#8217;s principal executive officer and principal financial officer, the effectiveness of the design and operation of its disclosure controls and procedures (as defined in Rule 13a-15(e) and 15d-15(e) under the Exchange Act) as of March&#160;31, 2026. Based on such evaluation, Magnolia&#8217;s principal executive officer and principal financial officer have concluded that as of such date, the Company&#8217;s disclosure controls and procedures were effective. The Company&#8217;s disclosure controls and procedures are designed to provide reasonable assurance that the information required to be disclosed by it in reports that it files under the Exchange Act is accumulated and communicated to management, including the Company&#8217;s principal executive officer and principal financial officer, as appropriate, to allow for timely decisions regarding required disclosure and is recorded, processed, summarized, and reported within the time periods specified in the rules and forms of the SEC.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-style:italic;font-weight:700;line-height:120%">Changes in Internal Control over Financial Reporting</span></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span><br/></span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:400;line-height:120%">There were no changes in the system of internal control over financial reporting (as defined in Rule 13a-15(f) and Rule 15d-15(f) under the Exchange Act) during the quarter ended March&#160;31, 2026 that have materially affected, or are reasonably likely to materially affect, the Company&#8217;s internal control over financial reporting.</span></div><div style="text-align:justify;text-indent:36pt"><span><br/></span></div><div id="i658097c8de274e8d8a7ff749ae5c7cb8_94"></div><div style="margin-top:12pt;text-align:center"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">PART II - OTHER INFORMATION</span></div><div><span><br/></span></div><div id="i658097c8de274e8d8a7ff749ae5c7cb8_97"></div><div style="text-align:justify"><span style="color:#000000;font-family:'Times New Roman',serif;font-size:10pt;font-weight:700;line-height:115%">Item 1.
